                                                                              Page 5 of 370


                    CITY OF LYNN HAVEN
           RECORDS RETENTION & DISPOSITION POLICY

1. STATUTORY AND REGULATORY AUTHORITY

Legal requirements regarding public records retention and disposition by
the City of Lynn Haven (the “City”) are governed by Chapter 119 and
Section 257.36 of the Florida Statutes.

Florida Statute Chapter 119 and Section 257.36 mandate that records must
be retained and disposed of only according to retention schedules
approved by the Division of Library and Information Services of the Florida
Department of State. Disposition of records for Local Government Agencies
requires adherence to Florida Department of State records management
program guidelines, which outline the General Records Schedule for State
and Local Government Agencies (GS1-SL).

2. STATE AND FEDERAL GRANT RETENTION REQUIREMENTS

In addition to statutory minimum retention schedules established by GS1-
SL, records associated with state, federal, or departmental grant programs
shall be maintained in accordance with the specific record retention
guidelines set forth by the applicable granting agency, grant agreement, or
governing state department.

In instances where a state department grant requirement, federal grant
rule, or contractual agreement dictates a retention period longer than the
minimum requirements under Florida Statute Chapter 119 or GS1-SL, the
City shall adhere to the longer retention period.

3. POLICY APPLICABILITY

The City of Lynn Haven shall adhere to Florida Statute Chapter 119 and
Section 257.36 in conjunction with GS1-SL, as well as all applicable state
and federal grant retention mandates when required, in the determination
and application of all records retention and disposition.

Board

The Planning Board consists of seven members appointed by
the City Commission to four-year terms and serves in an
advisory role. Members may serve up to two consecutive
terms. The Board coordinates the City’s planning efforts with
Bay County jurisdictions, monitors the City’s Comprehensive
Plan, and advises the City Commission on Comprehensive
Plan amendments, development orders, land use changes,
certificates of concurrency, and updates to the Unified Land
Development Code.

The purpose of this board is to study, investigate, and
recommend plans to the City Commission to enhance the
City’s beautification and cleanliness. Its duties include
promoting beautification efforts, encouraging the planting and
preservation of trees, plants, and other foliage, protecting
songbirds and their habitats, collaborating with local
organizations on beautification initiatives, and supporting the
Neighborhood Matching Grant Program.

This board advises the City Commission and Community
Services Department and builds partnerships to promote youth
and senior services. Duties include advising on project funding,
supporting fundraising, forming partnerships with leisure and
cultural organizations, exploring new programs, and promoting
events, capital improvements, and Leisure Services.

Consists of five members who are appointed by the City
Commission. Each Board Member sits for a three-year term,
and may serve two consecutive terms. Duties include: Act in an
advisory capacity to the City Commission and make
recommendations to the City. Commission concerning the
need to study, revise, or amend city codes and ordinances
affecting the growth and development of the City. Authorize
variances.

The purpose of this board is to examine qualifications of all
persons who desire to engage in the trade of business of
constructions. Duties include: Prescribe exams, Certificates of
Competency, notices of violation, conduct hearings, revoke or
suspend Certificates of Competency. Hear appeals of persons
charged with violations by contractors or persons who may be
aggrieved by the Building Official. Approve integrity of

Item # 1. Call to order.
Mayor Lowery called the meeting to order at 3:01P.M.
Item # 2. Invocation and the Pledge of Allegiance.
The Invocation was given followed by the Pledge of Allegiance.
Item # 3. Discussion on Decision dated 07/14/26 regarding RFP 25.26-17 Attorney Selection.
Mayor Lowery explained the process the Commission and City staff had undertaken to find City
Attorney Services. In light of the unanticipated issues that arose it has become evident that this
meeting was needed. It is his opinion that the decision made at the previous meeting needs to be
rescinded. Thereafter there were two options available. A.) To spend months going through a search
process and potentially another RFP or B.) pursuant to the City Charter, for the Commission to
appoint a new City Attorney firm by simple majority vote of the Commission.
Mayor Lowery invited Ms. Kayla Plat Rady (attorney Rumberger Kirk) to speak. Ms. Plat Rady read
some excerpts from the law and RFP regarding material changes which this appeared to be. She then
spoke about ways this can be cured. She recommended motion to rescind the previous decision and
then make a motion to look at next most qualified bidder, new RFP, or city commission to appoint
someone by simple majority vote.
Commissioner Peebles – so any way we go we have to rescind the previous decision? Yes.
Commissioner Peebles – was the main issue that the flat rate was offered after close of the bid? Yes,
also the clerical error was a material change.
Commissioner Warrick – this is our fault, because he stated at the last meeting that this was not a
minor error, and the city had emailed all commissioners explaining that. Did you not all get that
email? Commissioner Peebles – yes, but he wishes in that meeting someone would have stepped up
and said this is something we need to stop because it came in after the bid closed.
Mayor Lowery – apologized to Commissioner Warrick and explained he now understands the issue.
More discussion ensued.


       Motion by Commissioner Warrick        To rescind the prior selection of July 14, 2026, from
                                             RFP 25.26-17, without a formal award.


       Second to the Motion:                 Commissioner Tinder

11. Discussion and possible approval of SMART goals for the City Manager. (Commissioner
    Warrick)

                   AGREEMENT FOR MUNICIPAL LEGAL SERVICES


        THIS AGREEMENT FOR MUNICIPAL LEGAL SERVICES (“Agreement”) is
made and entered into this _____ day of ______________, 2026 (the “Effective Date”), by and
between: THE CITY OF LYNN HAVEN, FLORIDA, a Florida municipal corporation, acting
by and through its City Commission, whose address is 825 Ohio Avenue, Lynn Haven, Florida
32444 (hereinafter referred to as the “City”) and DUNLAP & SHIPMAN, P.A., a Florida
professional association, whose principal office is located at 2063 County Highway 395, Santa
Rosa Beach, Florida 32459 (hereinafter referred to as the “Attorney” or “General Counsel”), for
the provisions of legal services to the City. The City and the Attorney are sometimes individually
referred to herein as a “Party” and collectively as the “Parties.”
        NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual covenants and agreements set forth
herein, and for other good and valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency of which are
hereby acknowledged, the Parties agree as follows:
                                           ARTICLE I
                                     SCOPE OF SERVICES
Section 1.1. Appointment and General Duties
        The City hereby engages the Attorney to serve as General Counsel and City Attorney to
the City of Lynn Haven. The City Attorney shall represent the City as a municipal corporation,
with the City Commission serving as the governing body of the City. In this capacity, the Attorney
will provide legal advice and representation to the City in matters involving the City Commission,
City administration, and City departments, as appropriate and in accordance with applicable law.
        The City Attorney represents the legal interests of the municipal corporation as a whole
and does not represent individual elected officials, advisory board members, or employees in their
personal capacities. The Attorney shall serve as the primary legal advisor to the City Commission
and the City Staff, providing comprehensive municipal legal services as described herein,
consistent with the City Charter, Code of Ordinances, and applicable federal and state statutes and
regulations.
Section 1.2. Services
        The Attorney is expected to perform all general legal representation for the City and shall
provide legal services to the City including but not limited to the areas of legislative governmental
issues, land use and zoning, personnel, law enforcement, code enforcement, and contract law, as
well as monitoring the activities of any outside legal counsel representing the City in litigation.
       The Attorney shall provide the following legal services within the scope of this Agreement:
       (a) Attend all regular and special City Commission meetings and executive sessions. The
       Attorney shall additionally attend workshops, Planning Board meetings, Code
       Enforcement hearings, Community Redevelopment Agency (CRA) Board meetings, and
       other City board and committee meetings as specifically requested by the City Commission
       or City Manager. Requests for attendance under this subsection shall be made no later than
       48 hours in advance of the meeting except in the case of an emergency.



                                             Page 1 of 13


                                                                                                 Page 40 of 370


(b) Prepare, draft, and review ordinances, resolutions, policies, and procedures for
consideration by the City Commission, including all required legal research and risk
assessments;
(c) Render oral and written legal opinions as requested by the City Commission or City
Manager on matters affecting the City;
(d) Negotiate and prepare contracts, leases, agreements, deeds, easements, and other legal
instruments on behalf of the City;
(e) Review, edit, and comment upon contracts and agreements between the City and third
parties, including independent contractors and vendors;
(f) Perform legal research, investigations, witness interviews, and review of reports as
requested by the City Commission or City Manager;
(g) Provide legal counseling and guidance to the City Manager and department directors
on matters relating to City operations and administration;
(h) Assist with charter revisions, code amendments, referendum questions, and other
legislative and governance matters as requested;
(i) Provide staff assistance, legal research, and counseling related to real property
acquisition and disposition, leases, deeds, easements, utility franchise agreements,
referendum questions, contracts, surety, performance and payment bonds, insurance
policies, bills of sale, liens, waivers, subordinations, liability matters, grant guidelines and
compliance (including familiarity with programs such as CDBG and FEMA BRIC),
pension laws, arbitration, and collective bargaining;
(j) Assist in interpreting City ordinances and prosecute violations of local ordinances and
zoning regulations in cases where the State Attorney is not handling such prosecution;
(k) Maintain organized legal files and provide the City Manager with copies of
correspondence, pleadings, and orders in all litigation matters the firm is handling for the
City, together with periodic status updates no less frequently than quarterly (or more
frequently as requested by the City Commission or City Manager) to the City Manager to
keep the City informed of significant developments. Such updates are intended for
administrative purposes and shall not require presentation at a public meeting unless
specifically requested by the City;
(l) Keep the City Commission and City Manager informed of legislative enactments,
judicial opinions, and administrative rulings that have potential impact upon the City's
operations, finances, or governance;
(m) Oversee outside counsel retained by the City or appointed by the City's insurance
carriers in litigation, administrative proceedings, and bond issues, in a cost-effective
manner, as requested by the City;
(n) Provide independent legal advice to the City Commission on matters of governance,
compliance with the Government-in-the-Sunshine Law (Section 286.011, Florida
Statutes), ethics, and the statutory duties of elected officials;
(o) Handle litigation matters, including trial preparation, court appearances, depositions,
appellate work, and representation in administrative proceedings, as part of the Attorney's

                                      Page 2 of 13


                                                                                            Page 41 of 370


       comprehensive engagement under this Agreement, except matters in which outside counsel
       is retained by the City or through the City's insurance carrier or as otherwise provided in
       Article IV;
       (p) Handle labor and employment matters, including counseling, investigations, grievance
       proceedings, arbitration, and litigation arising from employment-related claims, except
       matters in which outside counsel is retained by the City or through the City's insurance
       carrier or as otherwise provided in Article IV; and
       (q) Perform such other professional legal duties, including legal research, as may be
       reasonably required to fulfill the Attorney's obligations as General Counsel to the City.
Section 1.3. Availability and Responsiveness
        The Attorney shall maintain regular communication with the City Commission, City
Manager, and designated City staff, and shall be reasonably available for consultation during
normal business hours. Normal business hours of the City of Lynn Haven are Monday through
Friday, between the hours of 7:30 am and 4:30 CDT, excluding City holidays. The City’s holidays
are posted on the City’s website under About Us / City Information / City Holidays.
Section 1.4. Coordination of Services
       The Attorney’s principal administrative contact with the City will be the City Manager and
City Clerk (and/or their designees). However, the City Attorney serves as legal counsel to the City
Commission and will also be available to provide legal advice to the City Commission when
requested. If the City hires in-house counsel, this individual will serve as the primary point of
contact, replacing the City Manager and City Clerk in that role.
                                           ARTICLE II
                                    TERM AND RENEWAL
Section 2.1. Initial Term
       This Agreement shall be for an initial term of one (1) year, commencing on the Effective
Date and expiring one year later, unless sooner terminated in accordance with Article VII of this
Agreement (the "Term").
Section 2.2. Renewal
        Upon expiration of the initial Term, this Agreement may be renewed for additional terms
upon the mutual written consent of the Parties, subject to approval by the City Commission and
annual budget appropriation. Either Party desiring to renew this Agreement shall provide written
notice of its intent to renew to the other Party no later than sixty (60) days prior to the expiration
of the then-current Term. Failure of the City to provide written notice of intent to renew within
such period shall not constitute an automatic termination but shall permit either Party to terminate
this Agreement upon its expiration without further obligation.
Section 2.3. Appropriations Clause
        The City's fiscal year runs from October 1 through September 30. The Parties acknowledge
that the City's performance and obligation to pay under this Agreement beyond the end of the
City's current fiscal year is contingent upon an annual appropriation of funds by the City
Commission in its adopted budget for each succeeding fiscal year. In the event that funds are not


                                             Page 3 of 13


                                                                                                  Page 42 of 370


appropriated for the services provided under this Agreement, the City shall notify the Attorney in
writing within thirty (30) days after the adoption of the budget for the succeeding fiscal year, and
this Agreement shall terminate on September 30 of the then-current fiscal year without penalty or
further obligation to the City, except for payment of services properly rendered through the
effective date of termination.
                                            ARTICLE III
                              COMPENSATION AND PAYMENT
Section 3.1. Annual Flat Fee
        In consideration of the services described in Article I, the City shall pay the Attorney an
all-inclusive annual flat fee of Two Hundred Seventy-Five Thousand and No/100 Dollars
($275,000.00) (the "Annual Fee"). The Annual Fee shall be paid in twelve (12) equal monthly
installments of Twenty-Two Thousand Nine Hundred Sixteen and 67/100 Dollars ($22,916.67)
each. The Annual Fee covers all services described in Section 1.2, including day-to-day legal
services, litigation, land development/redevelopment legal services, special projects, attendance at
meetings, general legal counsel, labor and employment matters, and all other legal services within
the Scope of Services.
         Routine office overhead expenses, including photocopying, word processing, facsimile,
standard postage, and local telephone charges, are included within the Annual Fee and shall not be
separately billed. Other than the pre-approved out-of-pocket expenses mentioned below, the
Attorney shall not charge for any other hours billed, expenses, or fees, regardless of the time
needed or complexity involved in any legal matter. Except as provided below, there shall be no
additional billing for any ancillary legal services, research expenses (such as Westlaw/LexisNexis
fees), travel, litigation, or miscellaneous costs or expenses.
Section 3.2. Invoicing and Payment
         The Attorney shall submit monthly invoices to the City in arrears, or as otherwise directed
by the City Manager, containing a general description of services rendered during the billing
period. Payment shall be due within thirty (30) days of the City's receipt and approval of a proper
invoice. Invoices shall be submitted in a format acceptable to the City. If an invoice remains unpaid
after sixty (60) days, the Attorney reserves the right to cease work until the account is made current.
Said accounts are also subject to the assessment of interest at the maximum legal rate beginning
sixty (60) days from the due date, in the Attorney’s discretion. Should the Attorney be required to
take legal action to collect any such unpaid fees and costs, the Attorney shall be entitled to its
collection fees and costs, including, but not limited to, its attorney’s fees and costs incurred in such
litigation.


Section 3.3. Reimbursable Expenses
        The following pre-approved out-of-pocket costs will be billed at cost, with no mark-up or
multiplier, and cleared in advance with the City Manager and/or his/her designee. The City shall
reimburse the Attorney for the following out-of-pocket costs and expenses reasonably incurred in
the performance of services under this Agreement:
       (a) Court filing fees and costs of service of process;


                                              Page 4 of 13


                                                                                                    Page 43 of 370


       (b) Courier fees;
       (b) Expert witness fees, subject to prior written approval by the City Manager;
       (c) Deposition transcript and court reporter fees;
       (d) Title search and recording fees; and
       (e) Large-scale outside reproduction costs (third party copying firm expenses).
Section 3.4. Books and Records
        The Attorney shall maintain adequate time records and documentation of services
performed and expenses incurred under this Agreement. Such books, records, and time
documentation shall be available for inspection and audit by the City, its authorized agents, or the
State of Florida Auditor General at any reasonable time during the term of this Agreement and for
a period of three (3) years following termination or expiration.
                                            ARTICLE IV
                    DUTIES AND OBLIGATIONS OF THE ATTORNEY
Section 4.1. Designated Attorneys
       The Attorney designates Courtney Sinagra, Esq. as the primary City Attorney and General
Counsel and principal point of contact for the City ("Primary Attorney"). Eric Krebs, Esq. shall
serve as secondary and supporting attorney. The Attorney's other qualified attorneys shall be
available to provide assistance as needed to fulfill the Attorney's obligations under this Agreement.
Any change in the designated Primary Attorney shall be communicated promptly to the City
Manager and shall be subject to the approval of the City Commission.
Section 4.2. Meeting Attendance
        The Primary Attorney shall personally attend all pre-scheduled City Commission meetings
(regular and special), workshops, and executive sessions. In the event the Primary Attorney is
unable to attend, Eric Krebs, Esq. shall attend, or, if neither is available, another qualified attorney
from the firm who is familiar with the City's matters shall attend, with advance coordination with
the City Manager. The Attorney shall attend in person where possible, or by telephone or electronic
means when in-person attendance is not feasible.
Section 4.3. Public Records Compliance
        In compliance with § 119.0701, Fla. Stat., and any other applicable provision of Chapter
119, the Attorney shall:
       (a)     Keep and maintain public records that would ordinarily and necessarily be required
       by the City in order to perform the services under this Agreement. Any documents created
       by the Attorney related to this contract shall be considered Public Record. This includes,
       without limitation, any and all financial, accounting, operational, or service records or
       reports kept, generated or issued as a normal part of the services provided;
       (b) Upon request from the City's custodian of public records, provide the City with a copy
       of the requested records or allow the records to be inspected or copied within a reasonable
       time at a cost that does not exceed the cost provided in Chapter 119, Fla. Stat., or as
       otherwise provided by law;


                                              Page 5 of 13


                                                                                                    Page 44 of 370


       (c) Ensure that public records that are exempt or confidential and exempt from public
       records disclosure requirements are not disclosed except as authorized by law for the
       duration of the Agreement term and following completion of the Agreement if the Attorney
       does not transfer the records to the City; and
       (d) Upon completion of the Agreement, transfer, at no cost, to the City all public records
       in possession of the Attorney or keep and maintain public records required by the City to
       perform the service. If the Attorney transfers all public records to the City upon completion
       of the Agreement, the Attorney shall destroy any duplicate public records that are exempt
       or confidential and exempt from public records disclosure requirements. If the Attorney
       keeps and maintains public records upon completion of the Agreement, the Attorney shall
       meet all applicable requirements for retaining public records. All records stored
       electronically must be provided to the City, upon request from the City's custodian of public
       records, in a format that is compatible with the information technology systems of the City.
       (e) If the Attorney does not comply with a public records request, the City shall be entitled
       to enforce these contract provisions by any legal or equitable means available, including,
       without limitation, damages, injunctive relief or both.
        Failure of the Attorney to abide by the terms of this provision shall be deemed a material
breach of this Agreement. This provision shall survive any termination or expiration of this
Agreement. In the event a civil action is filed against the Attorney to compel production of public
records where the Attorney has unlawfully refused to comply with the public records request
within the time required by law, the Plaintiff may be entitled to recover its reasonable costs of
enforcement, including reasonable attorney's fees from the Attorney as authorized by § 119.0701,
Fla. Stat.
        IF THE ATTORNEY HAS QUESTIONS REGARDING THE APPLICATION OF
CHAPTER 119, FLORIDA STATUTES, TO THE ATTORNEY'S DUTY TO PROVIDE
PUBLIC RECORDS RELATING TO THIS AGREEMENT, CONTACT THE
CUSTODIAN OF PUBLIC RECORDS AT: CITY CLERK, CITY OF LYNN HAVEN, 825
OHIO       AVENUE,       LYNN      HAVEN,      FLORIDA  32444 OR online at
https://cityoflynnhavenfl.nextrequest.com/requests/new.
Section 4.4. Disclosure of Conflicts
       The Attorney shall avoid conflicts of interest and shall comply with the conflict-of-interest
provisions of the Florida Rules of Professional Conduct. The Attorney has an ongoing obligation
to promptly disclose to the City Manager and City Commission any situation that, while acting
pursuant to this Agreement, would create an actual or potential conflict of interest between the
Attorney and their duties under this Agreement.
        The Attorney shall not be precluded from future representation of other clients whose
interests may be adverse to the City, provided that the City is no longer a client of the Attorney,
the averse matter is unrelated to matters addressed in this representation, and the future
representation does not conflict with any rule regulating the Florida Bar.
Section 4.5. Professional Conduct and Licensure
       All attorneys assigned to provide services under this Agreement shall be members in good
standing of The Florida Bar throughout the term of this Agreement and shall comply with the
Florida Rules of Professional Conduct. The Attorney shall promptly notify the City of any

                                            Page 6 of 13


                                                                                                Page 45 of 370


disciplinary action, suspension, or disbarment of any attorney providing services to the City under
this Agreement.
Section 4.6. Insurance
       The Attorney shall maintain, at its own expense, throughout the term of this Agreement,
insurance coverage in the following minimum amounts:
       (a) Commercial General Liability: $1,000,000 per occurrence / $2,000,000 aggregate;
       (b) Professional Liability (Legal Malpractice/Errors & Omissions): $2,000,000 per
       occurrence / $4,000,000 aggregate; and
       (c) Workers' Compensation: Statutory coverage as required by Chapter 440, Florida
       Statutes, with Employer's Liability limits of $1,000,000 each accident / $1,000,000 disease
       aggregate / $1,000,000 disease each employee.
        The City shall be named as certificate holder on all required policies. Certificates of
insurance shall be delivered to the City prior to the Effective Date of this Agreement. The Attorney
shall provide the City with at least thirty (30) days' advance written notice of any material change,
cancellation, or non-renewal of any required insurance policy.


Section 4.7. Anti-Discrimination Clause
        The Attorney shall comply with all applicable federal, state, and local laws, rules, and
regulations in the performance of services under this Agreement, including without limitation all
applicable nondiscrimination and equal opportunity laws and public contracting requirements.
Section 4.8. Use of E-Verify
       (a) Pursuant to § 448.095, Fla. Stat., effective July 1, 2020, the Attorney shall register with
           and use the U.S. Department of Homeland Security’s E-Verify system, https://e-
           verify.uscis.gov/emp to verify the employment eligibility of all employees hired during
           the term of this Agreement.
       (b) Subcontractors
               a. The Attorney shall also require all subcontractors performing work under this
                  Agreement to register with and use the E-Verify system for any employees they
                  may hire during the term of this Agreement.
               b. Subcontractors shall provide Contractor with an affidavit stating the
                  subcontractor does not employ, contract with, or subcontract with an
                  unauthorized alien, as defined by § 448.095, Fla. Stat.
               c. The Attorney shall provide a copy of such affidavit to the City upon receipt and
                  shall maintain a copy for the duration of the Agreement.
       (c) The Attorney must provide evidence of compliance with Fla. Stat. § 448.095.. Evidence
           may consist of, but is not limited to, providing notice of Contractor’s E-Verify number.
       (d) Failure to comply with this provision is a material breach of the Agreement, and the
           City may choose to terminate the Agreement at its sole discretion. The Attorney may



                                             Page 7 of 13


                                                                                                  Page 46 of 370


           be liable for all costs associated with the City securing the same services, including,
           but not limited to, higher costs for the same services and rebidding costs (if necessary).
Section 4.9. Human Trafficking
        When a contract is executed, renewed, or extended between a nongovernmental entity and
a governmental entity, the nongovernmental entity must provide the governmental entity with an
affidavit signed by an officer or a representative of the nongovernmental entity under penalty of
perjury attesting that the nongovernmental entity does not use coercion for labor or services as
defined in this section. For purposes of this subsection, the term "governmental entity" has the
same meaning as in § 287.138(1), Fla. Stat. A copy of the Attorney’s executed affidavit is attached
hereto.
Section 4.10. Prohibition on Contracting with Countries of Foreign Concern
       In accordance with § 287.138, Florida Statutes, the Attorney certifies that it:
       (a) Is not owned by the government of a foreign country of concern;
       (b) Does not have the government of a foreign country of concern as the controlling interest
           in Attorney; and
       (c) Is not organized under the laws of or has its principal place of business in a country of
           foreign concern.
Section 4.11. No Use of Forced Labor
         In accordance with § 287.1346, Florida Statutes, the Attorney acknowledges that it may
not use forced labor as defined in this section. By signing below, the Attorney certifies that the
commodities being offered to the City have not been produced, in whole or in part, by forced labor.
The Attorney further acknowledges that the use of forced labor as defined by § 287.1346, Fla.
Stat., and placement of the Attorney on the forced labor vendor list maintained by the Department
of Management Services is grounds for immediate termination of this Agreement at the option of
the City.
                                           ARTICLE V
                        DUTIES AND OBLIGATIONS OF THE CITY
Section 5.1. Cooperation
         The City shall cooperate with the Attorney and provide reasonable access to City facilities,
staff, documents, information, and records as are reasonably necessary for the Attorney to perform
the services required under this Agreement. City staff and officials shall respond to the Attorney's
reasonable requests for information in a timely manner.
Section 5.2. Designation of Contacts
        The City designates the City Manager and/or his/her designee as the primary administrative
point of contact and gatekeeper for coordinating legal service requests, prioritizing matters, and
directing the day-to-day workflow between the City and the Attorney. The City shall identify a
records custodian contact for public records matters related to this Agreement.




                                             Page 8 of 13


                                                                                                 Page 47 of 370


Section 5.3. Payment
        The City shall review and process proper invoices for payment within thirty (30) days of
receipt, in accordance with Article III and the Florida Prompt Payment Act, Section 218.70 et seq.,
Florida Statutes, to the extent applicable.
Section 5.4. Meeting Notices
       The City shall provide the Attorney with reasonable advance notice of all City Commission
meetings, board meetings, committee meetings, and other proceedings requiring the Attorney's
attendance, and shall provide the Attorney with relevant agenda materials and background
information in advance of such meetings to the extent practicable.
Section 5.5. Transition Cooperation
        In the event the City appoints in-house legal counsel or otherwise transitions legal services
to a successor attorney during or after the term of this Agreement, the City shall cooperate in good
faith with the Attorney to facilitate an orderly transition of pending matters, files, and
responsibilities.
Section 5.6. Decision-Making Authority
        The City Commission retains ultimate decision-making authority with respect to all legal
matters affecting the City. The Attorney shall advise and recommend courses of action but shall
not take material action or commit the City to any position in litigation or negotiations without
prior authorization from the City Commission or City Manager, as appropriate.
                                          ARTICLE VI
                                        TERMINATION
Section 6.1. Termination for Convenience
       Because the City Attorney serves as legal counsel to the governing body of the City, any
decision to terminate the Attorney's contract shall be made by the City Commission, acting as a
body, at a properly noticed meeting. The City Commission, acting as a body, may terminate this
Agreement for convenience, with or without cause, at any time upon thirty (30) days' prior written
notice to the Attorney, delivered by hand or by certified United States mail, return receipt
requested. Upon such termination, the Attorney shall be entitled to payment for all services
properly performed and reimbursable expenses incurred through the effective date of termination.
Section 6.2. Termination for Cause by the City
        The City Commission, acting as a body, may terminate this Agreement for cause upon
written notice to the Attorney specifying the nature of the claimed default or deficiency. The
Attorney shall have a reasonable cure period, not to exceed thirty (30) days from receipt of such
notice (or such longer period as the City may authorize in writing if the nature of the default
reasonably requires additional time), within which to cure the identified default. If the default is
not cured within the specified cure period, the City may declare this Agreement terminated by
delivering a final written notice of termination. In the event of termination for cause, the Attorney
shall be entitled to payment for services properly performed prior to the effective date of
termination, less any damages incurred by the City as a result of the Attorney's default. The City
may withhold payments as a setoff against such damages pending a final determination thereof.


                                             Page 9 of 13


                                                                                                 Page 48 of 370


Section 6.3. Termination by the Attorney
        The Attorney may terminate this Agreement upon thirty (30) days' prior written notice to
the City, delivered by hand or by certified United States mail, return receipt requested, subject to
compliance with the Florida Rules of Professional Conduct governing withdrawal from
representation of a client. The Attorney shall not withdraw from representation at a time or in a
manner that would materially prejudice the City's interests without reasonable efforts to mitigate
such prejudice.
Section 6.4. Obligations Upon Termination
       Upon termination or expiration of this Agreement for any reason:
       (a) The City shall pay the Attorney for all services properly performed and reimbursable
       expenses properly incurred through the effective date of termination, net of any amounts
       owed by the Attorney to the City;
       (b) The Attorney shall promptly deliver to the City or its successor counsel all City files,
       records, documents, work product, attorney-client privileged materials, and all other
       property of the City in the Attorney's possession or control, at no additional cost to the
       City;
       (c) The Attorney shall cooperate fully in an orderly transition of all pending matters to the
       City's successor counsel, including providing reasonable briefings on the status of pending
       matters and introducing successor counsel to relevant contacts and opposing parties as
       appropriate; and
       (d) The Attorney shall comply with all applicable provisions of the Florida Rules of
       Professional Conduct regarding the termination of the attorney-client relationship,
       including providing reasonable notice to courts, tribunals, and opposing counsel of record
       as required.
Section 6.5. Survival
       The following provisions shall survive the expiration or termination of this Agreement:
Sections 3.4 (Books and Records), 4.3 (Public Records Compliance), 4.7 (Indemnification), and
6.4 (Obligations Upon Termination), and any other provisions that by their nature are intended to
survive termination.
                                          ARTICLE VII
                              GOVERNING LAW AND VENUE
Section 7.1. Governing Law
        This Agreement shall be governed by and construed in accordance with the laws of the
State of Florida, without regard to its conflict-of-laws principles.
Section 7.2. Venue
       Any action or proceeding arising out of or relating to this Agreement shall be brought
exclusively in the state courts of Bay County, Florida, or, where federal subject-matter jurisdiction
is mandatory, in the United States District Court for the Northern District of Florida, Panama City




                                            Page 10 of 13


                                                                                                 Page 49 of 370


Division.1 Each Party hereby consents to the personal jurisdiction of such courts and waives any
objection to venue therein.
                                                ARTICLE VIII
                                   MISCELLANEOUS PROVISIONS
Section 8.1. Entire Agreement
       This Agreement, including any exhibits or attachments hereto, constitutes the entire
agreement between the Parties with respect to the subject matter hereof and supersedes all prior
and contemporaneous agreements, representations, negotiations, and understandings, whether
written or oral, relating to the subject matter of this Agreement. No reliance is placed on any
representation, warranty, or agreement made by either Party prior to the execution of this
Agreement.
Section 8.2. Amendments
       This Agreement may not be modified, amended, or supplemented except by a written
instrument duly executed by both Parties. Any amendment on behalf of the City must be approved
by the City Commission.
Section 8.3. Severability
       If any provision of this Agreement, or the application thereof to any person or
circumstance, is held invalid, illegal, or unenforceable by a court of competent jurisdiction, the
remainder of this Agreement, and the application of such provision to other persons or
circumstances, shall not be affected thereby, and each remaining provision shall be valid and
enforceable to the fullest extent permitted by law.
Section 8.4. Counterparts and Electronic Signatures
       This Agreement may be executed in any number of counterparts, each of which shall be
deemed an original, and all of which together shall constitute one and the same instrument.
Signatures transmitted by electronic means (including, without limitation, DocuSign, Adobe Sign,
facsimile, or PDF attachment to email) shall be deemed original signatures for all purposes.
Section 8.5. No Waiver
       The failure of either Party to enforce any provision of this Agreement shall not constitute
a waiver of such Party's right to enforce such provision or any other provision in the future. No
waiver of any breach or default shall be deemed a waiver of any subsequent breach or default of
the same or a different provision.
Section 8.6. Assignment
        The Attorney shall not assign, transfer, or delegate this Agreement or any rights, duties, or
obligations hereunder without the prior written consent of the City Commission, which consent
may be withheld in the City's sole discretion, given the personal and professional nature of the
legal services contemplated herein. Any purported assignment in violation of this Section shall be
void and of no force or effect.

Section 8.7. Independent Contractor
        The Attorney is and shall remain an independent contractor and is not an employee, agent,
partner, or joint venturer of the City. Nothing in this Agreement shall be construed as creating an
employer-employee relationship, partnership, or joint venture between the Parties. The Attorney
shall have no authority to bind the City or to incur any obligation on behalf of the City except as
expressly authorized herein or by the City Commission. The Attorney shall be solely and entirely
responsible for his or her acts during the performance of this Agreement.
Section 8.8. Notices
        All notices, requests, demands, and other communications required or permitted under this
Agreement shall be in writing and shall be deemed duly given when (a) delivered by hand; (b) sent
by certified United States mail, return receipt requested, postage prepaid; or (c) sent by nationally
recognized overnight courier service, in each case addressed as follows:
       If to the City:
               City Manager
               City of Lynn Haven
               825 Ohio Avenue
               Lynn Haven, Florida 32444
       If to the Attorney:
               Dunlap & Shipman, P.A.
               Attn: Gary Shipman, President
               2063 County Highway 395
               Santa Rosa Beach, Florida 32459

        Either Party may change its address for notices by providing written notice of such change
to the other Party in accordance with this Section.
Section 8.9. Compliance with Applicable Law
       The Attorney shall comply with all applicable federal, state, and local laws, statutes,
ordinances, rules, and regulations in the performance of services under this Agreement.
Section 8.10. No Third-Party Beneficiaries
        This Agreement is entered into solely for the benefit of the Parties hereto. Nothing in this
Agreement is intended to confer upon any person or entity other than the Parties any rights,
benefits, or remedies of any nature whatsoever.
Section 8.11. Construction
        This Agreement shall not be construed more strictly against one Party than the other merely
by virtue of the fact that it may have been prepared primarily by counsel for one of the Parties.
The Parties acknowledge that both Parties have contributed substantially and materially to the
preparation of this Agreement.

                          AGREEMENT FOR MUNICIPAL LEGAL SERVICES


        THIS AGREEMENT FOR MUNICIPAL LEGAL SERVICES (“Agreement”) is made and entered
into this _____ day of ______________, 2026 (the “Effective Date”), by and between: THE CITY OF LYNN
HAVEN, FLORIDA, a Florida municipal corporation, acting by and through its City Commission, whose
address is 825 Ohio Avenue, Lynn Haven, Florida 32444 (hereinafter referred to as the “City”) and DUNLAP &
SHIPMAN, P.A., a Florida professional association, whose principal office is located at 2063 County Highway
395, Santa Rosa Beach, Florida 32459 (hereinafter referred to as the “Attorney” or “General Counsel”), for the
provisions of legal services to the City. The City and the Attorney are sometimes individually referred to herein
as a “Party” and collectively as the “Parties.”
       NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual covenants and agreements set forth herein, and for
other good and valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency of which are hereby acknowledged, the Parties
agree as follows:
                                                   ARTICLE I
                                            SCOPE OF SERVICES
Section 1.1. Appointment and General Duties
        The City hereby engages the Attorney to serve as General Counsel and City Attorney to the City of Lynn
Haven. The City Attorney shall represent the City as a municipal corporation, with the City Commission serving
as the governing body of the City. In this capacity, the Attorney will provide legal advice and representation to
the City in matters involving the City Commission, City administration, and City departments, as appropriate and
in accordance with applicable law.
       The City Attorney represents the legal interests of the municipal corporation as a whole and does not
represent individual elected officials, advisory board members, or employees in their personal capacities. The
Attorney shall serve as the primary legal advisor to the City Commission and the City Staff, providing
comprehensive municipal legal services as described herein, consistent with the City Charter, Code of Ordinances,
and applicable federal and state statutes and regulations.
Section 1.2. Services
       The Attorney is expected to perform all general legal representation for the City and shall provide legal
services to the City including but not limited to the areas of legislative governmental issues, land use and zoning,
personnel, law enforcement, code enforcement, and contract law, as well as monitoring the activities of any
outside legal counsel representing the City in litigation.
       The Attorney shall provide the following legal services within the scope of this Agreement:
       (a) Attend all regular and special City Commission meetings and executive sessions. The Attorney shall
       additionally attend workshops, Planning Board meetings, Code Enforcement hearings, Community
       Redevelopment Agency (CRA) Board meetings, and other City board and committee meetings as
       specifically requested by the City Commission or City Manager. Requests for attendance under this
       subsection shall be made no later than 48 hours in advance of the meeting except in the case of an
       emergency.
       (b) Prepare, draft, and review ordinances, resolutions, policies, and procedures for consideration by the
       City Commission, including all required legal research and risk assessments;

Section 3.4. Books and Records
        The Attorney shall maintain adequate time records and documentation of services performed and expenses
incurred under this Agreement. Such books, records, and time documentation shall be available for inspection
and audit by the City, its authorized agents, or the State of Florida Auditor General at any reasonable time during
the term of this Agreement and for a period of three (3) years following termination or expiration.
                                                    ARTICLE IV
                             DUTIES AND OBLIGATIONS OF THE ATTORNEY
Section 4.1. Designated Attorneys
         The Attorney designates Courtney Sinagra, Esq. as the primary City Attorney and General Counsel and
principal point of contact for the City ("Primary Attorney"). Eric Krebs, Esq. shall serve as secondary and
supporting attorney. The Attorney's other qualified attorneys shall be available to provide assistance as needed to
fulfill the Attorney's obligations under this Agreement. Any change in the designated Primary Attorney shall be
communicated promptly to the City Manager and shall be subject to the approval of the City Commission.
Section 4.2. Meeting Attendance
        The Primary Attorney shall personally attend all pre-scheduled City Commission meetings (regular and
special), workshops, and executive sessions. In the event the Primary Attorney is unable to attend, Eric Krebs,
Esq. shall attend, or, if neither is available, another qualified attorney from the firm who is familiar with the City's
matters shall attend, with advance coordination with the City Manager. The Attorney shall attend in person where
possible, or by telephone or electronic means when in-person attendance is not feasible.
Section 4.3. Public Records Compliance
         In compliance with § 119.0701, Fla. Stat., and any other applicable provision of Chapter 119, the Attorney
shall:
                                                      Page 4 of 11




                                                                                                                                                             Page 58 of 370


       (a)     Keep and maintain public records that would ordinarily and necessarily be required by the City in
       order to perform the services under this Agreement. Any documents created by the Attorney related to
       this contract shall be considered Public Record. This includes, without limitation, any and all financial,
       accounting, operational, or service records or reports kept, generated or issued as a normal part of the
       services provided;
       (b) Upon request from the City's custodian of public records, provide the City with a copy of the requested
       records or allow the records to be inspected or copied within a reasonable time at a cost that does not
       exceed the cost provided in Chapter 119, Fla. Stat., or as otherwise provided by law;
       (c) Ensure that public records that are exempt or confidential and exempt from public records disclosure
       requirements are not disclosed except as authorized by law for the duration of the Agreement term and
       following completion of the Agreement if the Attorney does not transfer the records to the City; and
       (d) Upon completion of the Agreement, transfer, at no cost, to the City all public records in possession of
       the Attorney or keep and maintain public records required by the City to perform the service. If the
       Attorney transfers all public records to the City upon completion of the Agreement, the Attorney shall
       destroy any duplicate public records that are exempt or confidential and exempt from public records
       disclosure requirements. If the Attorney keeps and maintains public records upon completion of the
       Agreement, the Attorney shall meet all applicable requirements for retaining public records. All records
       stored electronically must be provided to the City, upon request from the City's custodian of public
       records, in a format that is compatible with the information technology systems of the City.
       (e) If the Attorney does not comply with a public records request, the City shall be entitled to enforce
       these contract provisions by any legal or equitable means available, including, without limitation,
       damages, injunctive relief or both.
        Failure of the Attorney to abide by the terms of this provision shall be deemed a material breach of this
Agreement. This provision shall survive any termination or expiration of this Agreement. In the event a civil
action is filed against the Attorney to compel production of public records where the Attorney has unlawfully
refused to comply with the public records request within the time required by law, the Plaintiff may be entitled to
recover its reasonable costs of enforcement, including reasonable attorney's fees from the Attorney as authorized
by § 119.0701, Fla. Stat.
        IF THE ATTORNEY HAS QUESTIONS REGARDING THE APPLICATION OF CHAPTER 119,
FLORIDA STATUTES, TO THE ATTORNEY'S DUTY TO PROVIDE PUBLIC RECORDS RELATING
TO THIS AGREEMENT, CONTACT THE CUSTODIAN OF PUBLIC RECORDS AT: CITY CLERK,
CITY OF LYNN HAVEN, 825 OHIO AVENUE, LYNN HAVEN, FLORIDA 32444 OR online at
https://cityoflynnhavenfl.nextrequest.com/requests/new.
Section 4.4. Disclosure of Conflicts
        The Attorney shall avoid conflicts of interest and shall comply with the conflict-of-interest provisions of
the Florida Rules of Professional Conduct. The Attorney has an ongoing obligation to promptly disclose to the
City Manager and City Commission any situation that, while acting pursuant to this Agreement, would create an
actual or potential conflict of interest between the Attorney and their duties under this Agreement.
        The Attorney shall not be precluded from future representation of other clients whose interests may be
adverse to the City, provided that the City is no longer a client of the Attorney, the averse matter is unrelated to
matters addressed in this representation, and the future representation does not conflict with any rule regulating
the Florida Bar.
Section 4.5. Professional Conduct and Licensure
       All attorneys assigned to provide services under this Agreement shall be members in good standing of
The Florida Bar throughout the term of this Agreement and shall comply with the Florida Rules of Professional

Conduct. The Attorney shall promptly notify the City of any disciplinary action, suspension, or disbarment of any
attorney providing services to the City under this Agreement.

Section 4.9. Human Trafficking
         When a contract is executed, renewed, or extended between a nongovernmental entity and a governmental
entity, the nongovernmental entity must provide the governmental entity with an affidavit signed by an officer or
                                                    Page 6 of 11




                                                                                                                                                        Page 60 of 370


a representative of the nongovernmental entity under penalty of perjury attesting that the nongovernmental entity
does not use coercion for labor or services as defined in this section. For purposes of this subsection, the term
"governmental entity" has the same meaning as in § 287.138(1), Fla. Stat. A copy of the Attorney’s executed
affidavit is attached hereto.
Section 4.10. Prohibition on Contracting with Countries of Foreign Concern
       In accordance with § 287.138, Florida Statutes, the Attorney certifies that it:
       (a) Is not owned by the government of a foreign country of concern;
       (b) Does not have the government of a foreign country of concern as the controlling interest in Attorney;
           and
       (c) Is not organized under the laws of or has its principal place of business in a country of foreign concern.
Section 4.11. No Use of Forced Labor
        In accordance with § 287.1346, Florida Statutes, the Attorney acknowledges that it may not use forced
labor as defined in this section. By signing below, the Attorney certifies that the commodities being offered to the
City have not been produced, in whole or in part, by forced labor. The Attorney further acknowledges that the use
of forced labor as defined by § 287.1346, Fla. Stat., and placement of the Attorney on the forced labor vendor list
maintained by the Department of Management Services is grounds for immediate termination of this Agreement
at the option of the City.
                                                  ARTICLE V
                               DUTIES AND OBLIGATIONS OF THE CITY
Section 5.1. Cooperation
        The City shall cooperate with the Attorney and provide reasonable access to City facilities, staff,
documents, information, and records as are reasonably necessary for the Attorney to perform the services required
under this Agreement. City staff and officials shall respond to the Attorney's reasonable requests for information
in a timely manner.
Section 5.2. Designation of Contacts
       The City designates the City Manager and/or his/her designee as the primary administrative point of
contact and gatekeeper for coordinating legal service requests, prioritizing matters, and directing the day-to-day
workflow between the City and the Attorney. The City shall identify a records custodian contact for public records
matters related to this Agreement.                                                                                      Commented [JW18]: The City Manager shall serve as the

Section 5.4. Meeting Notices
        The City shall provide the Attorney with reasonable advance notice of all City Commission meetings,
board meetings, committee meetings, and other proceedings requiring the Attorney's attendance, and shall provide
the Attorney with relevant agenda materials and background information in advance of such meetings to the extent
practicable.

        The City Commission, acting as a body, may terminate this Agreement for cause upon written notice to

by delivering a final written notice of termination. In the event of termination for cause, the Attorney shall be
entitled to payment for services properly performed prior to the effective date of termination, less any damages
incurred by the City as a result of the Attorney's default. The City may withhold payments as a setoff against such
damages pending a final determination thereof.
Section 6.3. Termination by the Attorney
        The Attorney may terminate this Agreement upon thirty (30) days' prior written notice to the City,
delivered by hand or by certified United States mail, return receipt requested, subject to compliance with the
Florida Rules of Professional Conduct governing withdrawal from representation of a client. The Attorney shall
not withdraw from representation at a time or in a manner that would materially prejudice the City's interests
without reasonable efforts to mitigate such prejudice.
Section 6.4. Obligations Upon Termination
       Upon termination or expiration of this Agreement for any reason:
       (a) The City shall pay the Attorney for all services properly performed and reimbursable expenses properly
       incurred through the effective date of termination, net of any amounts owed by the Attorney to the City;
       (b) The Attorney shall promptly deliver to the City or its successor counsel all City files, records,
       documents, work product, attorney-client privileged materials, and all other property of the City in the
       Attorney's possession or control, at no additional cost to the City;
       (c) The Attorney shall cooperate fully in an orderly transition of all pending matters to the City's successor
       counsel, including providing reasonable briefings on the status of pending matters and introducing
       successor counsel to relevant contacts and opposing parties as appropriate; and

                                                     Page 8 of 11




                                                                                                                                                         Page 62 of 370


        (d) The Attorney shall comply with all applicable provisions of the Florida Rules of Professional Conduct
        regarding the termination of the attorney-client relationship, including providing reasonable notice to
        courts, tribunals, and opposing counsel of record as required.
Section 6.5. Survival
      The following provisions shall survive the expiration or termination of this Agreement: Sections 3.4
(Books and Records), 4.3 (Public Records Compliance), 4.7 (Indemnification), and 6.4 (Obligations Upon
Termination), and any other provisions that by their nature are intended to survive termination.                                       Commented [JW20]: I didn’t see an Indemnification

                                                         ARTICLE VII
                                            GOVERNING LAW AND VENUE
Section 7.1. Governing Law
       This Agreement shall be governed by and construed in accordance with the laws of the State of Florida,
without regard to its conflict-of-laws principles.
Section 7.2. Venue
        Any action or proceeding arising out of or relating to this Agreement shall be brought exclusively in the
state courts of Bay County, Florida, or, where federal subject-matter jurisdiction is mandatory, in the United
States District Court for the Northern District of Florida, Panama City Division.1 Each Party hereby consents to
the personal jurisdiction of such courts and waives any objection to venue therein.
                                                        ARTICLE VIII
                                           MISCELLANEOUS PROVISIONS
Section 8.1. Entire Agreement
        This Agreement, including any exhibits or attachments hereto, constitutes the entire agreement between
the Parties with respect to the subject matter hereof and supersedes all prior and contemporaneous agreements,
representations, negotiations, and understandings, whether written or oral, relating to the subject matter of this
Agreement. No reliance is placed on any representation, warranty, or agreement made by either Party prior to the
execution of this Agreement.
Section 8.2. Amendments
       This Agreement may not be modified, amended, or supplemented except by a written instrument duly
executed by both Parties. Any amendment on behalf of the City must be approved by the City Commission.
Section 8.3. Severability
        If any provision of this Agreement, or the application thereof to any person or circumstance, is held
invalid, illegal, or unenforceable by a court of competent jurisdiction, the remainder of this Agreement, and the
application of such provision to other persons or circumstances, shall not be affected thereby, and each remaining
provision shall be valid and enforceable to the fullest extent permitted by law.
Section 8.4. Counterparts and Electronic Signatures
        This Agreement may be executed in any number of counterparts, each of which shall be deemed an
original, and all of which together shall constitute one and the same instrument. Signatures transmitted by
electronic means (including, without limitation, DocuSign, Adobe Sign, facsimile, or PDF attachment to email)
shall be deemed original signatures for all purposes.
Section 8.5. No Waiver

 The Parties understand that the Panama City Division Courthouse is closed indefinitely and United States District Court proceedings
may need to take place in Tallahassee, Florida.

                                                                                                                                                                    Page 63 of 370


        The failure of either Party to enforce any provision of this Agreement shall not constitute a waiver of such
Party's right to enforce such provision or any other provision in the future. No waiver of any breach or default
shall be deemed a waiver of any subsequent breach or default of the same or a different provision.
Section 8.6. Assignment
       The Attorney shall not assign, transfer, or delegate this Agreement or any rights, duties, or obligations
hereunder without the prior written consent of the City Commission, which consent may be withheld in the City's
sole discretion, given the personal and professional nature of the legal services contemplated herein. Any
purported assignment in violation of this Section shall be void and of no force or effect.
Section 8.7. Independent Contractor
       The Attorney is and shall remain an independent contractor and is not an employee, agent, partner, or joint
venturer of the City. Nothing in this Agreement shall be construed as creating an employer-employee relationship,
partnership, or joint venture between the Parties. The Attorney shall have no authority to bind the City or to incur
any obligation on behalf of the City except as expressly authorized herein or by the City Commission. The
Attorney shall be solely and entirely responsible for his or her acts during the performance of this Agreement.
Section 8.8. Notices
        All notices, requests, demands, and other communications required or permitted under this Agreement
shall be in writing and shall be deemed duly given when (a) delivered by hand; (b) sent by certified United States
mail, return receipt requested, postage prepaid; or (c) sent by nationally recognized overnight courier service, in
each case addressed as follows:
       If to the City:
               City Manager
               City of Lynn Haven
               825 Ohio Avenue
               Lynn Haven, Florida 32444
       If to the Attorney:
               Dunlap & Shipman, P.A.
               Attn: Gary Shipman, President
               2063 County Highway 395
               Santa Rosa Beach, Florida 32459

        Either Party may change its address for notices by providing written notice of such change to the other
Party in accordance with this Section.
Section 8.9. Compliance with Applicable Law
       The Attorney shall comply with all applicable federal, state, and local laws, statutes, ordinances, rules,
and regulations in the performance of services under this Agreement.
Section 8.10. No Third-Party Beneficiaries
       This Agreement is entered into solely for the benefit of the Parties hereto. Nothing in this Agreement is
intended to confer upon any person or entity other than the Parties any rights, benefits, or remedies of any nature
whatsoever.
Section 8.11. Construction
        This Agreement shall not be construed more strictly against one Party than the other merely by virtue of
the fact that it may have been prepared primarily by counsel for one of the Parties. The Parties acknowledge that
both Parties have contributed substantially and materially to the preparation of this Agreement.

Add Section 2.2. Annual Performance Review
The City Commission shall conduct an annual performance review of the Attorney using review criteria adopted by
the City Commission. Prior to the public discussion of the review, each Commissioner should be provided the
opportunity to complete an individual performance review using the adopted review criteria. The results of the
individual reviews should be shared with the Attorney in advance of the public meeting to encourage meaningful
discussion and continuous improvement.
The City Commission shall discuss the annual performance review at a duly noticed public meeting or workshop.
The review may include, but shall not be limited to:
(a) Responsiveness and availability to provide legal services;
(b) Attendance and preparedness for meetings and emergency matters;
(c) Quality, accuracy, and timeliness of legal advice;
(d) Communication with the City Commission, City Manager, City Clerk, and City staff, as appropriate;
(e) Judgment, professionalism, and risk management;
(f) Knowledge of municipal law and applicable legal requirements; and
(g) Compliance with the terms of this Agreement.
The purpose of the annual performance review is to evaluate the quality of legal services provided, promote
accountability, identify opportunities for improvement, and assist the City Commission in determining whether any
modifications to this Agreement or the professional relationship are in the best interests of the City.
The annual performance review shall be conducted for the purpose of continuous improvement and shall not, by
itself, create any contractual right to continued representation, compensation adjustment, or modification of this
Agreement.
The annual performance review shall not, by itself, constitute approval of any modification to the Annual Fee or any
other provision of this Agreement. Any modification to the compensation, scope of services, or other contractual
terms shall require separate approval by the City Commission through a written amendment to this Agreement.

Department: CMO                                    Prepared By:


Agenda Title: Discussion of directing staff on Resolution 2026-06-559 regarding consolidation
of services. (City Manager)


Requested Action: Direct staff on specifics of the Resolution for Consolidation of Services
Approved June 23, 2026.

I. Report in Brief:

On June 23, 2026, the Commission unanimously voted directing the City Manager to evaluate
opportunities for service consolidation funded through the General Fund.


Background:

The City Manager has been working at the direction of the Commission to evaluate the cost-
savings potential for consolidation of services of the General Fund, which include Animal
Control, Police, Fire, etc. After this approval and public discussion, it does not appear that this is
still the Commission's desire and other agencies are not interested in providing numbers for these
services. If the Commission wants to move forward with this, there needs to be a vote directing
staff on which services to evaluate specifically.

Recommendation:

Direct staff on the consolidation of services.

                      CITY OF LYNN HAVEN, FLORIDA
                      Commission Agenda Report
                      THE PUBLIC IS INVITED TO ATTEND




Department: Planning                           Prepared By: Gina Grandpre


Agenda Title: Final Reading of Ordinance 1176-AR Comprehensive Plan Amendment
(Planning Director)


Requested Action: Final Reading and Public Hearing of Ordinance 1176-AR

I. Report in Brief:

Ordinance 1176-AR is presented for second and final reading and adoption. It corrects the
Comprehensive Plan policies identified as conflicting with House Bill 180, readopts the
remaining amendments from Ordinance 1176, and incorporates the revisions requested by
Florida Commerce during state-coordinated review. Staff, Bay County GIS, and DesignWest, Inc.
have completed the updated Future Land Use Map Series, Future Transportation Map,
supporting data and analysis, and Infrastructure Element revisions. All objections,
recommendations, and comments identified by the State have been addressed.


Background:

Ordinance 1176, adopted on September 9, 2025, and ratified on October 14, 2025, amended
the City’s Comprehensive Plan. After the amendments were transmitted to the State, Florida
Commerce notified the City on December 4, 2025, that certain policies conflicted with HB 180
because they were considered more restrictive or burdensome.
Ordinance 1176-AR restores the identified policies to their prior language and readopts the
remaining amendments. The Planning Commission recommended approval on January 6, 2026,
and the City Commission held the first reading and transmittal public hearing on February 10,
2026.
On April 16, 2026, Florida Commerce requested an updated Future Land Use Map Series and
Future Transportation Map supported by data and analysis, along with revisions to the
Infrastructure Element. Staff, Bay County GIS, and DesignWest, Inc. completed the requested
corrections, which are included in the attached adoption package.




                                                                                          Page 70 of 370


Recommendation:

Planning Commission Recommendation: On January 6, 2026, the Planning Commission
recommended approval of Ordinance 1176-AR.

Recommendation and Alternatives: Staff recommends that the City Commission conduct the
public hearing, take public input, discuss Ordinance 1176-AR, and choose from the following
options.
Option 1 – Approval “I move to approve Ordinance 1176-AR on second and final reading, with
the Findings and Conditions in the Staff Report, and authorize staff to transmit the adopted
amendment package to Florida Commerce and the appropriate reviewing agencies.”

Findings

    1. Ordinance 1176-AR restores the identified policies to the language in effect before
       Ordinance 1176 and readopts the remaining Comprehensive Plan amendments.
    2. The Planning Commission recommended approval on January 6, 2026, and the City
       Commission held the first reading and transmittal public hearing on February 10, 2026.
    3. Florida Commerce reviewed Amendment 26-01ER through the state-coordinated review
       process and issued its report on April 16, 2026.
    4. The Future Land Use Map Series and Future Transportation Map have been updated
       using the data and analysis completed by DesignWest, Inc.
    5. The Infrastructure Element has been revised to address the applicable statutory
       requirements.
    6. The revisions address the objections, recommendations, and comments issued by
       Florida Commerce.
    7. Ordinance 1176-AR is supported by the attached data and analysis and is consistent
       with Chapter 163, Florida Statutes, and Chapter 2025-190, Laws of Florida.

       WHEREAS, Section 163.3191, Florida Statutes, requires that each local government adopt
an Evaluation and Appraisal Report (“EAR”) every seven years assessing the local government’s
progress in implementing the Comprehensive Plan; and

State and the reviewing agencies for comment under the State Coordinated Review Process; and

        WHEREAS, the City Commission held a properly advertised public hearing on September

Ordinance 1176-R with changes addressing the objection provided by the State reviewing agencies.
The adopted Ordinance 1176-R was transmitted to the State on September 30, 2025, but was
rejected by the State because the transmittal occurred more than 10 days following the
Commission’s action to adopt the amendments; and

       WHEREAS, at the State’s direction, the Commission held properly advertised public
hearing to consider a third reading and ratification of Ordinance 1176-R on October 14, 2025, which
was transmitted to the State on October 14, 2025; and

        WHEREAS, on December 4, 2025, the State rejected the City’s Ordinance 1176-R,

       SECTION 3. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause or phrase of the City’s

Comprehensive Plan shall be declared unconstitutional or unenforceable, such holding shall not

affect the remainder of this Ordinance.
